<meta>Handwritten name "Belser" on the draped cloth. Round library stamp "Mo. Bot. Garden 1890" below the date. Pencil shelf marks "QK 85 H47 c.2" in the top right margin.</meta>

<image-desc>An elaborate engraved architectural title page. The central title text is displayed on a large, draped curtain held between two ornate Corinthian columns. Above the curtain, a broken pediment supports a central coat of arms (belonging to the Prince-Bishop of Eichstätt), flanked by two reclining female allegorical figures: the one on the left holds a cornucopia filled with flowers and fruit, and the one on the right holds a beehive and an olive branch. Standing on pedestals in front of the columns are two statues of kings famous for their gardens: King Solomon on the left, holding a scepter, and King Cyrus of Persia on the right, holding a pruning tool. Between the pedestals is a landscape engraving depicting the Garden of Eden, featuring a central palm tree, a lion, an elephant, deer, rabbits, and two human figures. Vertical garlands of fruit and vegetables hang on the outer sides of the columns.</image-desc>

--- Page 3 ---
<lang>Latin</lang>
<page-type>frontispiece</page-type>
<meta>Engraved portrait of Marquard II Schenk von Castell (1605–1685), Prince-Bishop of Eichstätt. The date 1640 (CIƆ . IƆCXL) and the subject's age (35) are noted in the inscription. Ghosting of text from the facing page or verso is visible throughout the image.</meta>

<image-desc>
An elaborate copperplate engraving featuring a central oval portrait of a cleric, Marquard II, Bishop of Eichstätt. He is depicted with a mustache and goatee, wearing a dark ecclesiastical robe with a wide, flat white collar. 

The oval frame contains a Latin inscription: "REVERENDISSIMVS ATQVE ILLVSTRISSIMVS PRINCEPS AC DOMINVS DNS MARQVARDVS II. DG. EPISCOPVS EISTETTENSIS S.R.I. PRINCEPS AO XPI CIƆ . IƆCXL. AET. XXXV."

The portrait is set within a complex architectural and allegorical frame. At the top, three cherubs are present: the one on the left holds a sword and scales (Justice), and the one on the right holds a mirror and a snake (Prudence). 

In niches on either side of the portrait stand two allegorical female figures. The figure on the left holds a large tablet or book inscribed with "FIDES SPES CHARI." (Faith, Hope, and Charity). The figure on the right is Temperance, shown pouring liquid from a pitcher into a cup.

At the bottom center is an ornate coat of arms featuring four quarters with various charges, surmounted by a mitre and a crozier. This is flanked by two circular medallions (emblems). The left medallion shows clouds, the Hebrew Tetragrammaton (יהוה), and a curved agricultural tool, with the motto "IN DEO MEO". The right medallion shows a hand reaching from a cloud to hold a pair of dividers over a column, with the motto "IN PRINCIPE MEO".
</image-desc>

--- Page 4 ---
<lang>Latin</lang>
<page-type>frontispiece</page-type>
<warning>Significant bleed-through from the verso page visible in the background.</warning>

<image-desc>
A large, elaborate copperplate engraving featuring a central oval portrait of a bearded man in clerical or noble dress, identified by the inscription as Johannes Conradus, Bishop of Eichstätt. He wears a high, starched ruff collar and a richly textured doublet. The portrait is set within an architectural frame. At the top, putti (cherubs) hold a floral garland over the frame. To the left of the oval stands a female allegorical figure holding a set of chains; to the right stands another female figure holding a cornucopia overflowing with plants. Below the portrait is an ornate coat of arms surmounted by a bishop's mitre and a crozier. Flanking the coat of arms are two smaller circular emblems: the left depicts a table with several items and the motto "VINCIENDAE FORTVNAE"; the right depicts a landscape with crops and the motto "CORONANDAE VIRTVTI". The artist's signature "Wolfgang Kilian F." appears in small print below the engraving on the left.
</image-desc>

->REVERENDISSIMVS ATQVE ILLVSTRISSIMVS PRINCEPS AC DOMINVS D. IOANNES CONRADVS EPISCOPVS EYSTETTENSIS. ANNO 1606. ÆTATIS 45.<-

Wolfgang Kilian F.

--- Page 5 ---
<lang>Latin</lang>
<page-type>preface</page-type>

# SPECTATORI.

<image-desc>A large ornamental woodcut initial letter 'E' featuring intricate floral and scrollwork patterns within a square frame.</image-desc>

EXhibemus tibi per hasce tot paginas HORTVM EYSTETTENSEM ante annos aliquammultos propter ipsas radices ARCIS VVILIBALDINÆ extructum, SÆCVLO AVREO, ausu regio, impensis maximis, successu impræstabili, auspicijs Reuerendissimi atque Illustrissimi PRINCIPIS IOANNIS CONRADI. Sed, vt sunt humana, superueniens nuper ex AQVILONE PROCELLA decussit flores & proturbatos suis cum plantulis thalamos euertit; vt jam, quo loco olim Mitissimæ Stirpes adoleuerant, nihil aliud supersit, quam ferox & truculenta BELLONÆ Facies; Alibi superstantes saxis minacibus aggeres; alibi præcipitati defendendis aggeribus scopuli, & fulmina per obliquas rupes prospicientia, & trunci ac prominentes cippi, & corbes rudi fabulo trementes; ac quidquid in bellantium perniciem hodierni Æui Solertia excogitauit.

Cæterùm ne Immortalis Ausî Memoria omnis Posteritati efflueret, fecit IS quem exoratus iterum DEVS Mitigandis horum Temporum Calamitatibus indulsit Reuerendissimus atq; Illustrissimus PRINCEPS AC DOMINVS D. MARQVARDVS Episcopus Eistettensis. Eius Munere ac Sumptibus habes quæ Curis hisce Secundis elaborauimus ex voto & sententia eorum, qui hanc operam desiderare visi; quales fuêre in GALLIA, BELGICA, BRITANNIA complures.

Ordinem secuti sumus, qualem prima exempla præiuêre, nisi quod superuacuum credimus in tot manipulos hanc messem digerere; Tripartita tamen Serie prodeunt, vt Prima Classis Vernas Stirpes exhibeat, Altera Æstiuas, Vltima eas quæ vix præcipitante Autumno apud Nos excoqui possunt.

Descriptiones, quas alij parùm sinceras, alij parùm scitas nec suffecturas huius Sæculi impatientiæ existimârunt, omisimus; eò etiam maximè, ne exemplo priscorum artificum peccaremus, quibus necesse fuit artis suæ simplicitati adpositis bona fide Indicibus succurrere, & admoneri scilicet Populum, Bouem esse qui spectetur, non Hominem.

Cætera quæ hîc addi poterant, tu inspice, æstima, percurre, & Gratias Habe ILLVSTRISSIMO PRINCIPI MARQVARDO, cuius Decora Merentur pluribus etiam, quam quos hîc Leges, FLORIBVS Coronari.

-> ) ❊ ( <-
-> ❦ <-

<image-desc>A three-column index of botanical specimens organized by folio number. Large, faint text reading "TABVLAE PLANTARVM" is visible in the background, spanning across the middle of the page.</image-desc>

# CLASSIS VERNAE.

### Fol. 1.

1. Buxus. **Buxbaum.**
2. Syringa flore cæruleo. **Spaniſcher Syringbaum.**
3. Syringa flore cinerei coloris. **Aſchenfarber Spaniſcher Syringbaum.**

### Fol. 2.

1. Ruscus. **Meuſsdorn.**
2. Syringa Italica flore albo pleno. **Weiſſer Syringbaum mit vollen Blumen.**
3. Syringa flore albo ſimplici. **Weiſſer Syringbaum mit einfachen Blumen.**

### Fol. 3.

1. Arbor Judæ, Siliqua Silueſtris. **Judasbaum / Wild Johannesbrot.**
2. Vvifolium Cotyledon Silueſtre. **Ein Blat.**
3. Lunaria minor, ſeu Botrytis. **Mohnkraut / Mohnraute.**
4. Hepatica paluſtris. **Waſſer-Leberkraut.**

### Fol. 4.

1. Ceraſus flore pleno. **Gefüllte Kirſchenblühe.**
2. Ceraſus racemoſa Silueſtris. **Elſchern / wilde Traubenkirſchen / oder Vogelkirſchen.**
3. Summitates Piceæ. **Fichten / rothe Tannenblühe.**

### Fol. 5.

1. Poma flore multiplici, ſeu Pleniflora. **Gefüllte Depfelblühe.**
2. Lychnis viſcoſa Silueſtris, flore incarnato. **Wilde leibfarbe Nägelein.**
3. Rapunculus Silueſtris minor. **Wilde Rapunzeln / mit purpurweiſſen Blumen.**

### Fol. 6.

1. Piſtacia arbor. **Welſch-Pimpernüſſlein.**
2. Laurus cum flore. **Lorberbaum mit ſeiner Blühe.**
3. Amygdala cum flore. **Mandelbaum mit der Blühe.**

### Fol. 7.

1. Anagyris anguſtifolia. **Linſenbaum / Baumbonen.**
2. Anagyris latifolia. **Breitblätterichte Baumbonen.**

### Fol. 8.

1. Staphyllodendron, Nux Veſicaria. **Pimpernüſſlein.**
2. Clematis Daphnoides, flore albo. **Singrüen oder Beerwinck / mit weiſſen Blumen.**
3. Clematis Daphnoides flore cæruleo. **Blaw Singrüen.**
4. Clematis Daphnoides flore purpureo. **Purpurfarb Singrüen.**
5. Clematis Daphnoides flore purpureo pleno. **Voll purpurfarb Singrüen.**

### Fol. 9.

1. Xyloſteum alterum. **Beinhöltzlein.**
2. Xyloſteum floribus albis. **Gemein Beinhöltzlein mit weiſſen Blumen.**
3. Oxyacantha. **Saurach / Weinlegelein / Saurerdorn / Peiſſelbeer.**

### Fol. 10.

1. Sambucus arborea. **Gefüllte Schwelcken / oder Schneeballen.**
2. Cytisus. **Baumklee.**
3. Trifoliū arboreſcens. **Rundtblättericher Baumklee.**

### Fol. 11.

1. Cytisus Columellæ. **Rauher Baumklee.**
2. Cytisus Cluſij. **Baumklee Cluſij.**
3. Geniſta Scoparia. **Gelbe Pfriemen / oder Khunſchroten.**
4. Polemonium Monſpelienſe. **Baumichter Wiſenklee.**

### Fol. 12.

1. Colutea Veſicaria. **Welſch Linſenbaum / Schafflinſen / Wildſenetbaum.**
2. Cytiſus Tabernæmontani, Spicatus. **Der Geiſsklee Tabernæmontani.**

### Fol. 13.

1. Guaiacana. **Vermeinter Zurgelbaum.**
2. Liguſtrum. **Klein Weidenmundholtz / Beinhöltzle.**

### Fol. 14.

1. Ribes Minor fructu rubro. **Klein roth Johannes Träubel / ſo ſüſslich ſeyn.**
2. Ribes rubra vulgaris. **Gemein roth Johannis-Beerlein.**
3. Ribes fructu nigro. **Schwartze Gichtbeer / oder Johannis-Träubl.**
4. Ribes fructu rubro, maior. **Groſſ roth frembd Johannis-Träubl.**
5. Ribes fructu albo. **Weiſſ Johannis-Beerlein.**

### Fol. 15.

1. Squammata ſeu dentaria maior. **Ohne Blatt geſchuept Fremſamkraut.**
2. Dens Canis flore albo. **Schoſſwurz / Weiſſ Steulwurz.**
3. Dens Canis flore rubro. **Roth Schoſſwurz.**
4. Pulmonaria maculoſa. **Fleckende Lungenkraut / Hirſchmangold.**
5. Primula veris Silueſtris flore pallido. **Bleichgelbe frühe Schlüſſelblumen.**

### Fol. 16.

1. Auricula Vrſi flore albo. **Weiſſer Bergſanickel.**
2. Auricula Vrſi flore purpureo. **Bergſanickel mit brauner Blumen.**
3. Auricula Vrſi flore luteo. **Bergſanickel oder Fluſſblumen.**
4. Ariſtolochia flore albo. **Weiſſ Holtwurz.**
5. Ranunculus nemoroſus flore albo & pleno. **Weiſſ gefüllte Waldhenlein.**

### Fol. 17.

1. Ranunculus nemoroſus flore pleno. **Gefüllte weiſſe Waldhenlein.**
2. Ranunculus nemoroſus flore luteo. **Gelbe Waldhenlein.**
3. Naſturtium aquaticum flore pleno. **Gefüllter weiſſer Kreſſ oder Gauchblumen.**
4. Naſturtium aquaticum ſimplici flore. **Gauchblumen.**

### Fol. 18.

1. Viola martia purpurea multiplici flore. **Brauner gefüllter Mertzenviol.**
2. Viola martia alba, flore multiplici albo. **Weiſſer gefüllter Mertzenviol.**
3. Viola matronalis flore purpureo. **Brauner einfacher Mertzenviol.**
4. Viola martia flore albo. **Weiſſer einfacher Mertzenviol.**
5. Viola martia flore rubro purpuraſcente. **Einfacher Roſinrother Mertzenviol.**

### Fol. 19.

1. Doronicum. **Gembſenwurz.**
2. Gariophyllata montana. **Bergbenedictenwurz.**
3. Primula veris flore pleno. **Gefüllte weiſſen Schlüſſelblumen.**
4. Primula veris gemino flore. **Doppelt in einander wachſende Schlüſſelblumen.**

### Fol. 20.

1. Conſolida media. **Braun gulden Gunſel.**
2. Orobus Pannonicus. **Wildbe Vngäriſche Wicken.**
3. Lychnis Silueſtris. **Wilde Morgenröſſlein.**

### Fol. 21.

1. Lunaria græca perennis. **Groſſ weiſſ Griechiſche Violen.**
2. Lunaria græca annua. **Braun Mondt Violen.**

### Fol. 22.

1. Geranium muſcatum inodorum. **Biſem Storchnabel ohne Geruch.**
2. Geranium Batrachioides cæruleum. **Blaw Storchenſchnabel blumen.**
3. Geranium Batrachioides flore albo. **Weiſſ Storchnabel.**

### Fol. 23.

1. Geranium Anglicanum Variegatum. **Engliſch geſprengt Storchnabel.**
2. Geranium ſanguinarium maius. **Blutberg / Storchenſchnabelkraut / oder Gottes Gnad.**

### Fol. 24.

1. Geranium Moſcatum odoratum. **Biſem Storchenſchnabelkraut.**
2. Geranium Fuſcum. **Fremder Storchenſchnabel.**
3. Geranium Saxatile. **Berg Storchenſchnabel.**

### Fol. 25.

1. Thalictrum flore incarnato. **Wahlſamen.**
2. Thalictrum flore albo.
3. Fumaria tenuifolia flore niueo.

### Fol. 26.

1. Ranunculus albo flore pleno. **Weiſſ gefüllte Hanenfuſſ.**
2. Ranunculus flore globoſo. **Alpenhanenfuſſ.**
3. Ranunculus bulboſus maior. **Gelb gefüllter Hanenfuſſ / oder Geiſſblumen.**

### Fol. 27.

1. Ranunculus Illyricus. **Illyriſcher Hanenfuſſ.**
2. Ranunculus hortenſis flore albo. **Gefüllter gelber Hanenfuſſ.**
3. Anemone Silueſtris flore albo. **Weiſſe wilde Anemonen.**

### Fol. 28.

1. Ranunculus hortenſis erectus flore ſimplici luteo. **Auffrechtſtehender Garten Hanenfuſſ.**
2. Ranunculus montanus maior flore luteo. **Gemeiner einfacher ſüſſer gelber Ackerhanenfuſſ / oder Schmalzblumen.**
3. Ranunculus erectus minimus flore pleno luteo proliferus. **Groſſer Berghanenfuſſ mit gelben Blumen.**

### Fol. 29.

1. Ranunculus Asiaticus gumoſâ radice flore pleno ſanguineo prolifero. **Aſiatiſcher Hanenfuſſ / lenglechter Wurtzel / mit vollen vnd bluetrothen Blumen / deren eine durch die ander wächſt.**
2. Ranunculus Asiaticus grumoſâ radice flore pleno ruberrimo. **Aſiatiſcher Hanenfuſſ / mit lenglichten knöttichten Wurtzeln / vnd ſehr rothen Blumen.**
3. Ranunculus Creticus grumoſâ radice flore niueo. **Aſiatiſcher Hanenfuſſ / mit lenglichten knöttichten Wurtzeln vnd ſchneeweiſſen Blumen.**
4. Ranunculus Asiaticus grumoſâ radice flore phœniceo ruberrimo. **Aſiatiſcher Hanenfuſſ mit lenglichten knöttichten Wurtzeln vnd rothen einfachen Blumen.**
5. Ranunculus Asiaticus grumoſâ radice flore flauo rubris venis diſtincto. **Aſiatiſcher Hanenfuſſ / mit lenglichten knöttichten Wurtzeln.**

3. Pæonia Pumilis. Pæonia / so die kleinste ist / derhalben das Zwerglein genannt.

A full-page botanical engraving featuring three distinct plant specimens arranged horizontally. 
On the left is a tall branch of a lilac plant with large, heart-shaped leaves and dense, terminal clusters of small tubular flowers. It is labeled with the Roman numeral "III" and the text "Syringa flore lacteo." (White-flowered lilac).
In the center, partially overlap-ped by the lilac branches, is a shorter, bushier plant with many small, oval leaves arranged in opposite pairs along its stems, and tiny flowers in the leaf axils. It is labeled with the Roman numeral "I" and the text "Buxus." (Boxwood).
On the right is another tall branch of a lilac plant, structurally identical to the one on the left, with heart-shaped leaves and terminal flower clusters. It is labeled with the Roman numeral "II" and the text "Syringa flore caeruleo." (Blue-flowered lilac).
The engravings are highly detailed, showing leaf venation and the intricate structure of the blossoms.

<image-desc>
This is a detailed botanical copperplate engraving featuring four distinct plants, each depicted with its root system, leaves, and flowers. The arrangement is typical of early 17th-century florilegia, such as Basler's "Hortus Eystettensis".
- In the upper right (labeled III), there is a cluster of primroses ("Primula veris flore pleno") characterized by dense, multi-layered (double) flower heads and thick, rounded, crinkled leaves forming a basal rosette.
- In the center (labeled I), a tall plant ("Doronicum") rises with a straight stem, featuring heart-shaped or ovate leaves with wavy edges along the stalk and three large composite, daisy-like flower heads.
- On the left (labeled IV), another primrose variety ("Primula Veris gemino flore") is shown with long, lanceolate leaves with serrated margins; it has two distinct flower stalks emerging from the same base, each bearing an umbel of flowers.
- In the bottom right (labeled II), a smaller plant ("Cariophyllata montana") features three-lobed leaves and nodding, bell-shaped flowers on thin stems.
The entire composition is rendered with fine cross-hatching and line work to show texture and depth.
</image-desc>

Detailed botanical illustration of two varieties of Lunaria (Honesty) plants.
The left illustration (II) shows a plant with serrated, heart-shaped leaves, terminal clusters of small four-petaled flowers, and flat, circular to oval seed pods (silicles) containing visible seeds. The root system at the base is shown with a thickened, branching structure.
The right illustration (I) shows a similar plant with larger, more elongated, and more deeply serrated heart-shaped leaves. It features similar clusters of flowers, but the seed pods are more distinctly elliptical or oblong. The root system is depicted as a dense cluster of many thin, fibrous roots.
The entire plate is rendered in a fine engraving style with precise line work and hatching for shading and texture.
</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A detailed botanical copperplate engraving featuring three bulbous plants. The composition is symmetrical, with the bulbs and root systems shown at the bottom.

In the center is a smaller plant, labeled 'I', identified as 'Ornithogalum minus' (Star-of-Bethlehem). It has a small, round bulb with numerous fine roots and a slender stalk bearing several star-shaped, six-petaled flowers. Two long, narrow leaves curve upward from the base.

To the left is a larger plant labeled 'III', 'Hyacinthus orientalis mixtus'. It features a large, round bulb and broad, fleshy leaves that curve outward. A central stalk holds a dense raceme of bell-shaped flowers.

To the right is another large hyacinth labeled 'II', 'Hyacinthus orientalis variegatus'. It is similar in structure to the one on the left, with broad upright leaves and a dense cluster of bell-shaped flowers on a thick stalk. A single flower bud hangs down at the base of the inflorescence.

The leaves of the two hyacinths cross behind the central plant, filling the upper background of the plate.
</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A 17th-century copperplate botanical engraving from a florilegium, depicting four flowering bulbous plants in a symmetrical arrangement. 
Across the upper portion of the page are three varieties of Hyacinthus orientalis (Oriental Hyacinth), each shown with its large, layered bulb and extensive root system. 
- The center hyacinth (unnumbered) features bell-shaped flowers with distinct vertical stripes on the petals.
- The left hyacinth (labeled II) has dense clusters of double flowers (flore reduplici).
- The right hyacinth (labeled III) features bell-shaped flowers with petals that curve sharply backwards (reflexis).
Centered in the lower portion, between the hyacinth bulbs, is a single Crocus vernus (labeled IIII) with a small bulb, thin upright leaves, and one open flower. 
The plate is numbered "47" in the top right corner (handwritten) and again in the bottom right corner (printed).
</image-desc>

A detailed botanical engraving featuring three plant specimens, each meticulously rendered from root to flower. 

1. On the left (labeled III) is "Sparganium" (bur-reed). It features long, linear, sword-shaped leaves and two flowering stalks. The stalks bear multiple globular, spiky flower or seed heads. The plant is shown with a thick, fibrous root system.

2. In the center (labeled I) is "Orobanche" (broomrape). This parasitic plant is depicted with a bulbous, scaly tuberous base and a single, unbranched stem densely packed with small, lipped flowers. It lacks true green leaves.

3. On the right (labeled II) is "Cicuta maxima" (likely water hemlock or a similar large umbellifer). It features large, compound pinnate leaves and a tall stem topped with wide, flat-topped umbels of tiny flowers. The root is depicted as a thick, fleshy, carrot-like taproot with small fibrous roots extending from it.

<image-desc>A detailed botanical copperplate engraving featuring three distinct flowering plants, shown from their roots to their blossoms. The central plant (labeled I) is a Canterbury bell (Campanula medium), identified as "Medium flore purpureo," characterized by a thick, upright stalk bearing large, drooping bell-shaped flowers and lanceolate, serrated leaves. Flanking it are two slender plants of corn cockle (Agrostemma githago, formerly Pseudomelanthium). The plant on the left (labeled II) is "Pseudomelanthium flore rubescente" (reddish-flowered corn cockle), and the one on the right (labeled III) is "Pseudomelanthium flore albo" (white-flowered corn cockle). Both side plants exhibit narrow, linear leaves and five-petaled, star-like flowers.</image-desc>

<image-desc>A detailed botanical copperplate engraving depicting three distinct flowering plants from the Aconitum genus (monkshood). Each specimen is illustrated from its tuberous root system up to its flowering stalk.

1. On the left (III): A plant with deeply lobed, palmate leaves and a stalk of characteristic helmet-shaped flowers.
2. In the center (I): A plant with very finely divided, almost thread-like foliage and a dense spike of flowers. Its root system is particularly prominent.
3. On the right (II): A taller, more robust plant with broad, deeply cut palmate leaves and large, prominent hooded flowers.

The style is characteristic of 17th-century botanical works, utilizing fine line-work and hatching to show texture and form.</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A detailed botanical copperplate engraving from the early 17th century (likely from Besler's Hortus Eystettensis) featuring three varieties of columbine (genus Aquileia). The illustration depicts the plants in their entirety, including intricate fibrous root systems, stalks, deeply lobed leaves, and flowers in various stages of bloom.

- On the left (labeled II): A tall variety with numerous nodding, spurred flowers and detailed foliage. 
- In the center (labeled III): A variety with "stellate" (star-shaped) flowers that are upright, multi-petaled, and lack the characteristic spurs of the genus.
- On the right (labeled I): A tall variety similar to the first, with nodding, spurred flowers and complex leaf structures.

The style uses fine-line hatching for depth and texture on the petals and leaves.
</image-desc>

- On the left (labeled III) is an iris species with a small bulb, several long, narrow leaves, and a single iris blossom.

- On the right (labeled II) is another iris species, similar in form to the first but with variegated (striped) petals on its flower.
The plants are depicted realistically, including their bulbs and root systems.
</image-desc>

A detailed botanical copperplate engraving featuring three specimens of Lychnis coronaria (commonly known as Rose Campion). 

The central specimen is the most prominent, depicting a plant with a large cluster of lanceolate basal leaves and a branching root system. Several tall, branching stems rise from the base, each terminating in large, multi-petaled (double) flowers resembling roses. 

To the left is a smaller specimen, labeled "II.", with single, five-petaled flowers. The petals of these flowers are marked with small dots, corresponding to the "punctata" description in the text.

To the right is another smaller specimen, labeled "III.", also with single, five-petaled flowers, but these are plain and without dots, corresponding to "non punctata". 

All three specimens show the characteristic opposite leaf arrangement and ribbed, tubular calyxes of the genus. The engraving uses fine cross-hatching and linework for shading and texture.
</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A high-quality botanical engraving from the early 17th century (likely from Basilius Besler's "Hortus Eystettensis") depicting three varieties of "Flos Constantinopolitanus" (Lychnis chalcedonica, or Maltese Cross). 

The three plants are arranged side-by-side, each shown in full from its fibrous rhizome root system up to its crowning floral cluster. The stalks are tall and straight, bearing pairs of clasping, lance-shaped leaves with distinct veining. Each plant is topped with a dense, flat-topped cyme of distinctive flowers, each petal having a deep notch at the tip giving it the appearance of a cross.

- The central plant (labeled I) is the "miniatus" (vermilion or scarlet) variety.
- The plant on the left (labeled II) is the "incarnatus" (flesh-colored or pink) variety.
- The plant on the right (labeled III) is the "albus" (white) variety.
</image-desc>

->II.<-

<image-desc>
A detailed botanical engraving depicting three distinct plants of the valerian family, arranged side-by-side. 

In the center, labeled "I," is a "Valeriana maior." It features a thick, gnarled, horizontal rhizome at the base with numerous fibrous roots extending downwards. Two thick, cut stems emerge from the rhizome, alongside a central tall stalk. The stalk has several pairs of opposite, pinnate leaves and terminates in a large, dense, rounded cluster (umbel) of small flowers, flanked by two smaller lateral flower clusters.

On the left, labeled "II," is "Valeriana Græca flore albo." It has a more slender stalk and finely divided, pinnate leaves. The root system is fibrous but less substantial than the central plant. The stalk is topped with an elongated, loose cluster of five-petaled white flowers.

On the right, labeled "III," is "Valeriana Græca flore coeruleo." This plant is morphologically similar to plant II, with a thin stalk and pinnate leaves, but its flowers are arranged in tighter, more rounded clusters at the apex, representing the blue-flowered variety.

The entire illustration is executed in fine black-and-white line work, emphasizing the textures of the roots, the veins of the leaves, and the structure of the flower clusters.
</image-desc>

<image-desc>A detailed botanical woodcut illustration depicting three distinct plant species. 
1. On the left (labeled III): A thistle-like plant characterized by a tall, spiny stem and large, serrated, spiny leaves. It has several large, rounded, brush-like flower heads. A detached, thick root system is shown at the bottom left base.
2. In the center (labeled I): A tall plant with long, lanceolate leaves along its stem. It features a single large, apical flower head with long, needle-like florets or bracts, and several smaller buds. The root is shown as fibrous.
3. On the right (labeled II): A plant with deeply lobed, pinnatifid leaves and multiple smaller, oval-shaped flower heads on a branching upper stem. Its root system is also fibrous.

<image-desc>
A full-page, highly detailed botanical engraving from the early 17th century (likely from Besler's "Hortus Eystettensis"). The plate depicts two different species of thistle. 

On the left, identified as "Acantium Sylvestre", is a tall plant with a slender, straight stem covered in dense, sharp thorns. Its leaves are deeply lobed and serrated, with each point ending in a sharp spine. The plant features three flower heads: one fully developed at the top left, a smaller bud at the top right, and another emerging from a lower leaf axil. 

On the right, identified as "Carduus Sphærocephalus", is a more robust plant with thick, sturdy stems. Its leaves are large, broad, and undulating, also featuring sharp spines along the margins. The plant is notable for its large, perfectly spherical flower heads that have a dense, spiky texture, resembling a mace. There are three primary flower heads shown at the top of branching stems. 

The engraving uses fine hatching and cross-hatching to create a sense of three-dimensional form and realistic texture for the stems, leaves, and bristly flowers.
</image-desc>

A detailed botanical engraving from a historical herbal, featuring three distinct plants arranged horizontally across the page. 
In the center, labeled "I.", is a Sea Holly (Eringium marinum), a robust plant with thick, spiny stems and large, prickly, lobed leaves. It has several globose, thistle-like flower heads, each surrounded by a ring of sharp, radiating bracts. 
To the left, labeled "II", is a yellow-flowered Rock Rose (Helianthemum), depicted with a tall, slender stem, small oval leaves in opposite pairs, and several five-petaled blossoms. 
To the right, labeled "III.", is a white-flowered Rock Rose (Helianthemum), sharing a similar structure to the yellow variety with thin stems and delicate five-petaled flowers, though with slight variations in the foliage and bud clusters. 
The roots of all three plants are shown at the bottom.

Three detailed botanical copperplate engravings showing the whole plant, including root systems. 
1. On the left (marked III): Ptarmica Vulgaris. It features multiple slender stems rising from a small root cluster, with narrow, serrated leaves and several small, daisy-like flowers at the terminal ends.
2. In the center (marked I): Papaver spinosum. A stout plant with a single thick stem and large, deeply lobed, spiny leaves. It bears a single open flower at the top and two prominent, spiny seed pods (capsules). The taproot is thick and tapering.
3. On the right (marked II): Chrysocome Peregrina. A more branched, bushy plant with numerous thin stems and small, linear leaves. The flower heads are small, round clusters grouped at the ends of the upper branches.

<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>illustration</page-type>
<columns>2</columns>
<page-num>157</page-num>

<image-desc>
A detailed 17th-century botanical engraving featuring two varieties of poppies. 
On the left (labeled I) is the "Papaver flore pleno rubrum" (double-flowered red poppy). It is dominated by a very large, dense, globular flower head composed of many fringed, overlapping petals. Below the main bloom is a seed pod with its characteristic radial crown. The leaves are large, glaucous, and deeply lobed with serrated margins, clutching the thick central stem. To the immediate right of this stem is a separate detailed illustration of its thick, tapering taproot.
On the right (labeled II) is the "Papaver Erraticum rubrum" (common wild red poppy). This specimen is more slender, featuring two open flowers with four large, delicate petals and a central cluster of stamens. It also shows a drooping, hairy bud and a more fibrous root system at the base. The leaves are smaller and more finely divided (pinnatifid) compared to the double poppy. 
The engraving uses fine line work and hatching for shading, typical of high-quality early modern botanical plates.
</image-desc>

### rubrum.

<page-num>168</page-num>
<meta>Botanical engraving; likely from Besler's Hortus Eystettensis (1613). Includes a small 'N' above the handwritten page number.</meta>

<image-desc>A detailed botanical engraving featuring three flowering plants including their roots. 
1. On the left (II), a climbing vine with large heart-shaped (cordate) leaves and several large trumpet-shaped flowers, shown winding around a vertical support.
2. In the center (I), a slender, upright plant with narrow lanceolate leaves and a central stalk from which several small bell-shaped flowers hang.
3. On the right (III), a climbing vine with trilobed leaves and trumpet-shaped flowers, also winding around a support.
The engraving shows intricate details of leaf venation, the twisting of the vines, and the fiber of the root systems.</image-desc>

A finely detailed botanical copperplate engraving depicting three distinct plants with their stems, leaves, flowers, and fruit. 

The central plant (labeled I) is a climbing vine with delicate tendrils and tri-lobed leaves. It features small flowers and inflated, heart-shaped or triangular seed pods. One pod near the bottom is shown partially open, revealing three small, round seeds within.

The plants on the left (labeled II) and the right (labeled III) have sturdier stems and large, ovate, pointed leaves. They are characterized by hanging, lantern-like inflated calyxes (husks), characteristic of the Physalis genus. These husks are depicted with detailed venation.

This is a detailed black-and-white botanical engraving of two species of chili pepper plants (Piper Indicum), likely from Basilius Besler’s "Hortus Eystettensis" (1613). 

On the left (labeled II) is a plant with many slender, elongated peppers growing vertically ("erectum"). The plant is shown in its entirety, including a fibrous root system at the base, broad ovate leaves, and small, star-shaped flowers with five petals.

On the right (labeled I) is a similar plant but with much larger, rounded, heart-shaped fruits that also grow in an upright position. This specimen also includes a full root system, leaves, and flowers. 

The engraving style is refined, with hatched shading to provide three-dimensional depth to the leaves and fruits.

<image-desc>
A detailed botanical engraving from the early 17th century depicting three different plant species. 

In the center is the largest plant (labeled I), characterized by large, broad, ovate leaves with prominent veining at its base. A thick central stem rises to support a large, complex inflorescence composed of numerous thick, textured, finger-like flower spikes (panicles) that curve upward and outward.

To the upper left is a smaller plant (labeled II) featuring a basal rosette of deeply lobed, lanceolate leaves. Several slender, leafless stalks emerge from the center, ending in composite flowers that resemble dandelions, some in full bloom and others in bud. The root system is shown with fine fibrous roots.

To the upper right is a slender, delicate plant (labeled III) with a small rosette of narrow, simple leaves at the base. It has a tall, thin, branched stem bearing many tiny, star-shaped flowers on its upper portion. 
</image-desc>

A finely detailed botanical copperplate engraving from the early 17th century, likely from Basilius Besler's "Hortus Eystettensis". The composition features three plants arranged symmetrically.

<image-desc>
A page from a botanical work featuring three detailed engravings of plants with their root systems. 
- At the top center is "Aconitum hyemale" (Winter Aconite), shown with several stems bearing multi-petaled flowers, lobed leaves, and a small tuberous root with fibrous rootlets.
- At the bottom left is "Petasites flore albo" (White Butterbur), depicted with a tall, conical flower spike, emerging leaves at the base, and a thick horizontal rhizome from which many long roots descend.
- At the bottom right is "Petasites" (Common Butterbur), showing a similar tall flowering stalk with bracts and a large rhizome with a wide-spreading network of roots.
The labels for each plant include Roman numerals: I for Petasites, II for Aconitum hyemale, and III for Petasites flore albo.
</image-desc>

->II.<-
->Aconitum hyemale.<-

->III.<-
->Petasites flore albo.<-

->I.<-
->Petasites.<-

<image-desc>A botanical engraving featuring five different plants, each showing the flowers, foliage, and root systems. 
1. Top left (III): Pulsatilla flore albo, characterized by its hairy stems and large white flowers.
2. Top right (II): Pulsatilla flore cœruleo, showing similar hairy leaves and nodding bell-shaped flowers.
3. Center (I): Tussilago, featuring several scaly stems topped with small flower heads and a prominent taproot.
4. Bottom left (V): Saxifraga aurea, with low-growing, rounded, lobed leaves and clusters of small flowers.
5. Bottom right (IIII): Ranunculus nemorosus albus, displaying deeply divided leaves and multiple open flowers.</image-desc>

<image-desc>A botanical engraving featuring four detailed plant specimens, each labeled with a Roman numeral and its Latin name. 
1. Plant I (Aristolochia Vulgaris): Located in the center, it shows a plant with a rounded tuberous root, lobed leaves, and distinct curved, hooded flowers.
2. Plant II (Laureola): On the right, depicting a woody shrub (spurge-laurel) with small flowers appearing along the upper stems and a horizontal, spreading root system.
3. Plant III (Cornus): On the left, a tall, slender woody branch (dogwood) terminating in several clusters of small, delicate flowers.
4. Plant IIII (Crocus Vernus): In the lower-left corner, a spring crocus showing its corm (bulb), fibrous roots, narrow leaves, and a single upright star-shaped flower.</image-desc>
